CRWD — Evidence appendix
All harvested proof points · company first, then peers

Evidence

Claims by section
business-model
  • One agent, the Falcon sensor, is installed on a customer's laptops, servers, virtual machines, containers and domain controllers; it streams filtered telemetry to CrowdStrike's cloud, blocks attacks locally, and keeps working offline. Thirty-three cloud modules run on that one agent, and a customer buys more of them over time without deploying anything new
  • About 63% of ending ARR at July 2026 and about 68% of revenue once professional services is included.
  • Subscription cost of revenue was $1,016m in FY2026, 22.3% of subscription revenue. The FY2026 increase of $181m decomposes into cloud hosting +$46.4m, employee-related +$43.1m on 16% higher average headcount, data-centre depreciation +$33.4m, internal-use software amortisation +$24.9m, stock compensation +$18.1m and allocated overhead +$16.8m (FY2026 10-K, Cost of Revenue). Hosting and depreciation, the volume-driven items, were 44% of the increase.
  • About 37% of ending ARR at July 2026 and about 32% of revenue. Three lines with three pricing units, sold onto the same sensor through the same channel.
  • Next-Gen SIEM bills per gigabyte per day of third-party data ingested. CrowdStrike-generated telemetry is not charged and every Falcon Insight customer gets 10 GB/day of third-party ingestion free
  • A customer commits a dollar pool for one to five years at a pre-negotiated rate card across every module, draws it down as it activates modules, endpoints or workloads, trials modules in-app, and "re-Flexes" by adding to the pool.
  • At January 2023 the 436 accounts above $1m of ARR were 1.9% of customers and 46% of ARR.
capitalization
  • Unrecognised stock compensation was $2,880m at July 31, 2026 over about 2.5 years, up $822m in six months because H1 FY2027 granted 12,323k post-split units at a $112.13 weighted grant-date fair value against 9,916k units for all of FY2026, a six-month unit count 24% above the prior full year.
competitive-advantage
  • CrowdStrike's endpoint franchise earns high cash returns on a balance sheet funded by customer prepayments, and its installed base has survived two well-funded attempts to break it.
  • The mechanism is a sensor on every device a customer owns, a telemetry and label corpus a competitor cannot buy, a 24/7 human detection layer that also produces the training labels, and a module cross-sell that raises the cost of leaving with every module added: 51% of subscription customers use six or more modules at Q2 FY2027, up from 24% at FY2021.
  • There is no margin premium over the closest platform peer: GAAP operating margin was (6.1)% in FY2026 against Palo Alto Networks' 13.5% in FY2025
  • And cash conversion declined for five years: free cash flow went from 34.7% of revenue in FY2021 to 27.2% in FY2026 on operating cash flow less purchases of property and equipment, the peer-comparable basis used in the tables below, and from 33.5% to 25.7% on the company's own definition, which also deducts capitalised internal-use software; free cash flow after stock compensation went from 17.6% to 4.4% of revenue on the first basis.
  • ROIC peaked at 23.0% in FY2024 and has declined 2.8 points since, because invested capital before deferred revenue grew 79% over two years while non-GAAP NOPAT grew 59%, and $747m of the $2,086m increase is goodwill and intangibles from Adaptive Shield, Onum, Pangea and the FY2026 deals.
  • CrowdStrike grows faster than Palo Alto, Fortinet and Okta on the latest full year, spends more of revenue on R&D than any peer except SentinelOne, has no gross-margin premium, and trails every profitable peer on GAAP operating margin and free cash flow.
  • Datadog is the closest match: a single agent installed fleet-wide, telemetry pooled in the vendor's cloud, modules cross-sold on that agent, and multi-product adoption as the disclosed KPI (55% of customers on four or more products and 33% on six or more at December 31, 2025, Datadog FY2025 10-K), with the same exposure to a platform owner's native tools. It breaks as an analogue because monitoring does not hold a security control's 97% gross retention. ServiceNow matches the land-and-expand shape of one platform sold as successive workflows, with a 98% renewal rate in each of 2023-2025, and breaks because no supplier of ServiceNow's runtime is also its largest competitor. Verisk matches the pooled-contributory-data claim and breaks because Verisk's contributors cannot rebuild the dataset, whereas Microsoft observes every Windows endpoint without a third-party sensor. Of the three, the evidence supports a moat of Datadog's strength.
  • The three emerging lines inherit the endpoint franchise's advantage and add little of their own. Each is a module switched on for a sensor the customer already runs, and combined ending ARR rose from more than $850m at January 2024 to more than $2.18bn at July 2026, 2.6x in two and a half years.
  • The advantage is defensible for now and open to erosion, and it is weaker than the endpoint franchise's on three counts. In none of the three lines does CrowdStrike hold the position it holds on the endpoint.
  • Third, no cost advantage has appeared: GAAP subscription gross margin was 77.7-78.0% in each of FY2024, FY2025 and FY2026 while the trio rose from 25% to 36% of ARR, and the "collect once, monetize many times" claim is a FY2029 target (82-85% non-GAAP subscription gross margin), not a reported result.
  • The attack that has been tried and not repelled is Wiz's agentless posture model, which took the posture layer from 2021 onward. Kurtz's own description: CSPM "was an easy button for a lot of customers. And certainly, vendors had success in that area" (Q2 FY2026 call). CrowdStrike kept the runtime layer, reports "multiple Wiz replacements" (Q3 FY2026 call) and is a Frost Radar leader in cloud workload protection for the fourth consecutive time, and now sells both layers. Both vendors roughly doubled over two years, Wiz from $500m to above $1bn and CrowdStrike's cloud line from above $400m (January 2024) to above $800m (January 2026). Two scaled vendors with the larger one growing at least as fast, and a hyperscaler owning the larger one, is a contested duopoly.
competitive-landscape
  • CrowdStrike stopped citing an IDC share after the Q4 FY2023 call in March 2023 and now cites Gartner Magic Quadrant placement and an "endpoint security ISV" share, a category that excludes Microsoft.
  • Three years of continuous displacement by two vendors have not changed the stated figure, so either the estimate is loosely maintained or new endpoints land on legacy tools as fast as old ones leave.
  • Its single-quarter net new ARR of $333m in Q2 FY2027 exceeded SentinelOne's net new ARR for all of FY2026.
  • CrowdStrike is not the scale leader in any of the three.
  • The gap is closing fastest in identity and SIEM against the incumbents and widening in cloud against Wiz.
  • CrowdStrike's published total addressable market has been marked up at every briefing: $25bn at the IPO (2019), $36bn (CY2021), $58bn (CY2022), $76bn (CY2023), $100bn (CY2024), $116bn (CY2025), $140bn (CY2026) and $149bn (CY2026, restated in the Q4 FY2026 deck).
  • And CrowdStrike's own share of its own near-year TAM has barely moved: $1.73bn of ARR against a $58bn CY2022 figure is 3.0%; $5.84bn against $149bn is 3.9%
  • At July 31, 2025 management estimated logo penetration below 40% of the Global 2000, about 7% of firms with 251-7,499 employees, below 1% of the more than 50m businesses with 5-250 employees, and below 2% of the public sector
critical-points
  • and its FY2031 illustrative range of $2.5-3.0bn needs a 25-30% compound rate from $905m over four and a half years, which is today's rate with no further deceleration allowed. At the current dollar add the line reaches about $1.8bn by January 2031, $0.7bn below the bottom of that range.
  • Free cash flow less stock compensation was $143m in FY2021 and about $140m in FY2026; per share it declined from about $0.16 to about $0.14 post-split, having peaked at about $0.30 in FY2024.
  • the FY2027 guided non-GAAP operating margin of 25.0% includes $85-95m from lengthening commission amortisation from four to five years ($53.4m realised in H1), worth about 1.5 points on the revenue guide
  • Conversion to Flex lifts an account's ending ARR by more than 40% on average, a first re-Flex adds 25% about eight months later, and accounts that have re-Flexed twice run 53% above their initial Flex. A re-Flex books expansion into current-period net new ARR ahead of the renewal date, so part of FY2027's step is renewal timing.
expectations
  • Management, consensus and the base rate agree on the current year to within a rounding error, because consensus is management's guidance typed in. They diverge on how fast growth fades after FY2028 and on whether stock compensation ever converges toward what the software reference class does.
  • The price has moved on the multiple, not the estimates. Over the twelve months to 2026-09-02 the shares rose 99% while FY2027 revenue estimates rose 2.9%, FY2027 non-GAAP EPS 5.4% and FY2028 revenue 3.5%.
financial-history
  • From 35.5% (FY2017) to 74.7% (FY2026), and flat in a 73-75% band since FY2021. The subscription line does the work and has itself been flat at 76-78% for six years.
  • Services gross margin declined from 33.4% (FY2022) to 19.0% (FY2025), 17.9% (FY2026) and 10.3% in Q2 FY2027; had services held its FY2024 31.8% margin, FY2026 gross profit would have been $34.4m higher, worth 0.7 of a point of consolidated gross margin.
forward-model
  • Realized incremental non-GAAP operating margin (the change in non-GAAP operating income over the change in revenue) was 23.2%, 20.2%, 37.4%, 24.5% and 19.4% in FY2022-FY2026, and 41.3% in H1 FY2027; excluding the $53.4m of realised commission benefit, the H1 figure is about 32%.
  • the base reaches $10bn of ending ARR in FY2030, a year before the stated FY2031 date, and the bull in FY2029; the $20bn FY2036 goal is met only in the bull.
m-and-a
  • CrowdStrike has been a disciplined acquirer on every available outcome test, and the disclosures do not allow the most recent $1.5bn to be assessed.
  • Preempt at $91.2m for about $6.8m of ARR became a line above $585m; Humio at $370.3m plus a $57.2m tax charge for about $3.6m of ARR became a line above $695m.
management-claims
  • A data advantage that improves detection and retention without producing price increases or share gains against the largest rival is a real advantage of a bounded kind.
  • Net new ARR per dollar of the prior year's sales and marketing spend went from 1.70x (FY2022) to 1.34x, 0.97x, 0.71x and 0.66x (FY2026). The FY2027 guide midpoint implies 0.74x, the first improvement in five years and still below half the FY2022 level.
  • Microsoft applies the same structure with its own logs, and Palo Alto with firewall and Cortex data, so the mechanism is available to two of the three largest rivals and unavailable to Splunk and Elastic.
management-incentives
  • Against that model, free cash flow margin has moved away from the target: 30.7% (FY2024), 26.9%, 25.7% (FY2026), 28.1% for the twelve months to July 2026, against a 34-38% FY2029 target and an at-least-30% FY2027 guide.
secular-trends
  • Core net new ARR grew 81% year over year while trio net new ARR grew 33%. The core supplied 48% of the last twelve months' additions against 40% the year before, and its own growth rate went from 11.2% to 18.3%.
  • So the 52% splits into roughly 12-13 points of market growth, about 11 points of the listed next-generation cohort taking spend from legacy vendors, in-house budgets and services, and about 22 points of CrowdStrike taking share inside that cohort. Share gain inside the cohort was the largest single contributor.
  • In the latest comparable quarter that rate has halved. Cohort revenue including CrowdStrike grew 25.9% and CrowdStrike's share went from 16.67% to 16.64%; removing the $388m of Chronosphere and CyberArk revenue that Palo Alto's 10-Q discloses, the cohort grew 20.0% and CrowdStrike's share rose 0.79 of a point. Over FY2020-FY2025 the average annual gain was 1.74 points. The reason is that the cohort accelerated alongside CrowdStrike.
  • ARR is the forward run-rate that revenue converges toward, so revenue cannot compound faster than ARR indefinitely, and today's 25-26% revenue growth is catching up to an ARR base compounding at 25%.
  • The cloud market rose 58% with no change to the illustrative scale, and the SIEM market tripled with a $0.5bn increase in scale. On management's own two slides the assumed share of its own addressed markets by FY2031 falls by a third in cloud and by more than half in SIEM.
  • From the FY2027 guide midpoint of $6.61bn, $10bn by January 2031 needs a 10.9% compound rate and average net new ARR of about $850m a year over FY2028-FY2031, which is 37% below the FY2027 guide.
technology
  • What makes the sensor hard to copy is fourteen years of Windows-kernel engineering against an operating system whose interfaces change, plus the install base that makes the collected data useful.
  • A user-mode platform would lower the crash blast radius for every vendor, and it would also lower the kernel-engineering barrier that has protected the incumbents.
  • What a competitor would also need is the labelling: Falcon Complete, OverWatch and the incident-response team produced more than 15 million labelled annotations and more than 800 incident-response engagements in the twelve months to September 2025
  • Every Falcon customer's endpoint, identity and cloud telemetry is already in the platform, so a SIEM migration starts with roughly 80% of the volume in place and the customer pays only for the rest.
  • Palo Alto's XSIAM shows the same construct can be built from a firewall-plus-endpoint footprint: it reached more than $700m of ARR growing 70% with about 1,000 customers in FY2026 (PANW Q4 FY2026 call, 2026-09-01) [web], level with CrowdStrike's more than $695m.
value-chain
  • Amazon Web Services hosts the platform, is the largest single route to market, is a Falcon Complete customer, and competes through GuardDuty and Security Hub. Microsoft supplies the Windows kernel interfaces, sells the substitute inside E5, and from FY2027 also resells Falcon against Azure consumption commitments.
  • The channel keeps roughly a dime to twenty cents, CrowdStrike keeps roughly eighty cents of which about two-thirds is gross profit, and the hosting layer receives about a dime. Microsoft takes nothing from this dollar and sells its own bundle against it.
  • Subscription gross margin went from 36% (FY2017) to 78% (FY2024-FY2026) while AWS list prices did not move in CrowdStrike's favour in any disclosed way. The margin came from dual-sourcing between AWS and owned colocation, smart filtering, and modules after the first being close to pure gross margin.
  • The owned-colocation model has not produced a gross-margin gap against the AWS-only peers. CrowdStrike's subscription gross margin of 78% GAAP compares with Okta at 80% and Elastic at 81%, both on AWS, and with Zscaler at 77% on its own colocation; the gap it has produced is against SentinelOne at 74%, whose cost base is AWS usage charges at a fifth of CrowdStrike's scale. Scale, module attach and smart filtering explain more of the 78-81% than colocation does.
  • Concentration is the lowest in the peer set. No channel partner or direct customer was 10% or more of revenue or receivables in FY2024-FY2026.
  • The constraint now is contractual. About $7.0bn of non-cancelable commitments against $5.0bn of cash and $1.2bn of FY2026 free cash flow is a fixed cost base being laid down ahead of the growth that fills it, with the composition undisclosed. The exposure is the gross margin: hosting and data-centre depreciation supplied 42-44% of the increase in subscription cost of revenue in FY2026 and Q2 FY2027, and the FY2029 subscription gross-margin target of 82-85% assumes the capacity is used.

One agent, the Falcon sensor, is installed on a customer's laptops, servers, virtual machines, containers and domain controllers; it streams filtered telemetry to CrowdStrike's cloud, blocks attacks locally, and keeps working offline. Thirty-three cloud modules run on that one agent, and a customer buys more of them over time without deploying anything new

Company

earnings callsupports
So on the first point, it is separate and incremental. It's another module and it's priced separately, which is, again, where we're seeing a lot of the growth. When we think about the beauty of the architecture and the platform, it's still using the same agent. And if you want to enable AIDR, again, that's a license entitlements part of our friction-free deployment. Again, why is Falcon Flex so successful? Why have we been successful in media time to value, and you don't have to roll out yet another agent.
— George Kurtz, Co-Founder, President and CEO, CrowdStrike Q2 FY2027 earnings call, 2026-08-26
filingsupports
Our single lightweight agent is installed on each endpoint and provides local detection and prevention capabilities while also intelligently collecting and streaming high fidelity data to our platform for real-time decision-making. Today, we offer 11 cloud modules on our Falcon platform via a SaaS subscription-based model that spans multiple large security markets, including endpoint security, security and IT operations (including vulnerability management), and threat intelligence.
— CrowdStrike FY2020 Form 10-K (first 10-K after the June 2019 IPO), 2020-03-23
filingsupports ✦ best
We designed an intelligent lightweight sensor that is installed on each endpoint or cloud workload. Our sensor is capable of acting autonomously and continues to collect data and protect workloads running on endpoints even when offline. The sensor recommences transmitting data to our Falcon platform when the connection to the cloud has been re-established. The sensor is hardened against attacks and uses a combination of kernel and user-mode modules to collect and transmit high fidelity endpoint events as they take place on a system.
— CrowdStrike FY2026 Form 10-K (fiscal year ended January 31, 2026), 2026-03-05
filingsupports
Reducing Sensor Bloat: Our single intelligent lightweight sensor enables frictionless deployment of our platform at scale, enabling customers to rapidly adopt our technology across any type of workload running on a variety of endpoints. The sensor is non-intrusive to the end user, requires no reboots and continues to protect the endpoint and track activity even when offline. Through our single lightweight sensor approach, customers can adopt multiple platform modules to address their critical areas of risk without burdening the endpoint with multiple sensors. Legacy approaches often require multiple sensors as they layer on new capabilities.
— CrowdStrike FY2026 Form 10-K (fiscal year ended January 31, 2026), 2026-03-05
filingqualifies
Our cloud-native Falcon platform integrates seamlessly with our single lightweight sensor to deliver robust functionality across key areas of cybersecurity and IT operations. The Falcon platform delivered 32 cloud modules as of January 31, 2026 and currently delivers 33 cloud modules, enabling customers to address their most critical areas of risk with speed, confidence, and visibility through one unified platform.
— CrowdStrike FY2026 Form 10-K (fiscal year ended January 31, 2026), 2026-03-05
filingsupports
We can rapidly and cost effectively develop and deliver additional cloud modules on our Falcon platform without the need for additional sensors, and are expanding options for our new customers to test modules on a trial basis as well as offering in-application trials for existing customers. Our expanding set of open APIs and the Foundry app development platform allow customers and partners to build their own capabilities on top of the Falcon platform.
— CrowdStrike FY2026 Form 10-K (fiscal year ended January 31, 2026), 2026-03-05
filingsupports
Our growth will depend in part on our ability to continue to expand our relationships with our customers by deploying on additional endpoints in their environment and cross-selling more cloud modules. When customers deploy our lightweight sensor, they can easily add additional cloud modules. We also offer in-application trial usage of additional modules to cross-sell to existing customers. The power of our land-and-expand strategy is evidenced by our 115% dollar-based net retention rate as of January 31, 2026.
— CrowdStrike FY2026 Form 10-K (fiscal year ended January 31, 2026), 2026-03-05
filingcontradicts
Even if customers choose to renew their subscription of certain cloud modules, they may decline to purchase additional cloud modules or choose not to consolidate onto our Falcon platform. Any such impacts on customer renewals may result from a variety of different factors, including customers electing to renew with shorter subscription periods, fewer cloud modules, fewer endpoints or smaller contract values.
— CrowdStrike FY2026 Form 10-K (fiscal year ended January 31, 2026), 2026-03-05
filingqualifies
Our subscriptions are priced based on the unit of measure most relevant to each product, including per-endpoint, per-identity, per-cloud sensor, per-user, per-device, and per-gigabyte of daily ingestion. We recognize revenue from our subscriptions ratably over the term of the subscription.
— CrowdStrike Q2 FY2027 Form 10-Q (quarter ended July 31, 2026), 2026-08-27

filingsupports ✦ best
Subscription cost of revenue increased by $181.3 million, or 22%, in fiscal 2026, as compared to fiscal 2025. The increase in subscription cost of revenue was primarily due to an increase in cloud hosting and related services costs of $46.4 million, an increase in employee-related expenses of $43.1 million driven by a 16% increase in average headcount, an increase in depreciation of data center equipment of $33.4 million, an increase in amortization of internal-use software of $24.9 million, an increase in stock-based compensation expense of $18.1 million, an increase in allocated overhead costs of $16.8 million, and charges related to the Strategic Plan of $3.4 million, partially offset by a decrease in other labor expenses of $2.8 million and a decrease in company events expenses of $1.2 million.
— CrowdStrike FY2026 Form 10-K (fiscal year ended January 31, 2026), 2026-03-05

Datadog is the closest match: a single agent installed fleet-wide, telemetry pooled in the vendor's cloud, modules cross-sold on that agent, and multi-product adoption as the disclosed KPI (55% of customers on four or more products and 33% on six or more at December 31, 2025, Datadog FY2025 10-K), with the same exposure to a platform owner's native tools. It breaks as an analogue because monitoring does not hold a security control's 97% gross retention. ServiceNow matches the land-and-expand shape of one platform sold as successive workflows, with a 98% renewal rate in each of 2023-2025, and breaks because no supplier of ServiceNow's runtime is also its largest competitor. Verisk matches the pooled-contributory-data claim and breaks because Verisk's contributors cannot rebuild the dataset, whereas Microsoft observes every Windows endpoint without a third-party sensor. Of the three, the evidence supports a moat of Datadog's strength.

Peer: Palo Alto Networks

earnings callqualifies

The attack that has been tried and not repelled is Wiz's agentless posture model, which took the posture layer from 2021 onward. Kurtz's own description: CSPM "was an easy button for a lot of customers. And certainly, vendors had success in that area" (Q2 FY2026 call). CrowdStrike kept the runtime layer, reports "multiple Wiz replacements" (Q3 FY2026 call) and is a Frost Radar leader in cloud workload protection for the fourth consecutive time, and now sells both layers. Both vendors roughly doubled over two years, Wiz from $500m to above $1bn and CrowdStrike's cloud line from above $400m (January 2024) to above $800m (January 2026). Two scaled vendors with the larger one growing at least as fast, and a hyperscaler owning the larger one, is a contested duopoly.

Our NGS ARR represents the annualized allocated revenue of all active contracts as of the final day of the reporting period related to all product, subscription and support offerings, excluding revenue from hardware products, and legacy attached subscriptions, support offerings and professional services. Peer: Okta

filingqualifies
Sales commissions earned by the Company's sales force are generally considered incremental and recoverable costs of obtaining a contract with a customer. Sales commissions for new revenue contracts, including incremental sales to existing customers, are deferred and then amortized on a straight-line basis over a period of benefit, which is determined to be generally five years. The Company determined the period of benefit by taking into consideration the terms of its customer contracts, its technology life and other factors.
— Okta (10-K), Okta Form 10-K, fiscal year ended January 31, 2026, 2026-01-31

Peer: SentinelOne

filingqualifies
Commissions paid, including certain incentives paid to partners, on a new contract are amortized on a straight-line basis over an estimated period of benefit of four years, while commissions paid on renewal contracts are amortized over the average contractual term of the renewal. We determine the estimated period of benefit based on both quantitative and qualitative factors, including the duration of our relationships with customers and the estimated useful life of our technology.
— SentinelOne (10-K), SentinelOne Form 10-K, fiscal year ended January 31, 2026, 2026-01-31

filingqualifies
Commissions paid upon the initial acquisition of a contract are amortized over an estimated period of benefit of five years while commissions paid for renewal contracts are amortized over the contractual term of the renewals. Conversion to Flex lifts an account's ending ARR by more than 40% on average, a first re-Flex adds 25% about eight months later, and accounts that have re-Flexed twice run 53% above their initial Flex. A re-Flex books expansion into current-period net new ARR ahead of the renewal date, so part of FY2027's step is renewal timing.

Peer: Elastic

filingsupports
We generate revenue primarily from sales of subscriptions to our platform. We offer various paid subscription tiers that provide different levels of rights to use proprietary features and access to support. ... Our subscription agreements are either term-based or consumption-based, with the vast majority of Elastic Cloud subscriptions being consumption-based. ... Elastic Cloud customers may also purchase subscriptions on a month-to-month basis without a commitment, with usage billed at the end of each month.
— Elastic N.V. Form 10-K for fiscal year ended April 30, 2026, 2026-04-30
filingsupports
Because we recognize revenue under a consumption-based arrangement based on actual customer consumption, we do not have the same ability to predict the timing of revenue recognition as we do under subscription arrangements in which revenue is recognized on a predetermined schedule over the subscription term. Moreover, customers may consume our products at a different pace than we expect. For example, we have experienced and, if adverse economic conditions persist, may continue to experience slowing consumption as customers look to optimize their usage.
— Elastic N.V. Form 10-K for fiscal year ended April 30, 2026, 2026-04-30

ARR is the forward run-rate that revenue converges toward, so revenue cannot compound faster than ARR indefinitely, and today's 25-26% revenue growth is catching up to an ARR base compounding at 25%.

filingsupports ✦ best
ARR is calculated as the annualized value of our customer subscription contracts as of the measurement date, assuming any contract that expires during the next 12 months is renewed on its existing terms. To the extent that we are negotiating a renewal with a customer after the expiration of the subscription, we continue to include that revenue in ARR if we are actively in discussion with such organization for a new subscription or renewal, or until such organization notifies us that it is not renewing its subscription.
— CrowdStrike Holdings, Inc., CrowdStrike 10-K for fiscal year ended January 31, 2026, 2026-03-05
filingsupports
Subscription revenue primarily consists of subscription fees for our Falcon platform and additional cloud modules that are supported by our cloud-based platform. Subscription revenue is driven primarily by the number of subscription customers, the number of endpoints per customer, and the number of cloud modules included in the subscription. We recognize subscription revenue ratably over the term of the agreement, which is generally one to three years. We generally invoice our subscription customers at the beginning of the subscription term, or in some instances, such as in multi-year arrangements, in installments. Consequently, a substantial portion of the revenue that we report in each period is attributable to the recognition of deferred revenue relating to subscriptions that we entered into during previous periods.
— CrowdStrike Holdings, Inc., CrowdStrike 10-K for fiscal year ended January 31, 2026, 2026-03-05
filingqualifies
existence at the beginning of the period, as well as contract renewals and new customer contracts during the period, backlog at the beginning of any period is not necessarily indicative of future revenue performance. We do not utilize backlog as a key management metric internally.
— CrowdStrike Holdings, Inc., CrowdStrike 10-K for fiscal year ended January 31, 2026, 2026-03-05
filingqualifies
Our dollar-based net retention rate can fluctuate from period to period due to large customer contracts in a given period and incentives provided, which may reduce our dollar-based net retention rate in subsequent periods. In addition, if our customers are not able to fully utilize their product subscriptions (including in connection with our flexible subscription offering), we may experience increased contraction as such customers may elect to renew with shorter subscription periods, fewer cloud modules, fewer endpoints or smaller contract values, which may reduce our dollar-based net retention rate.
— CrowdStrike Holdings, Inc., CrowdStrike 10-K for fiscal year ended January 31, 2026, 2026-03-05

competitive-position · technology · ↩ back to dossier

Company

earnings callsupports
We have best-in-class architecture, and we lead the industry for a reason. We have greater efficacy, greater manageability and greater scalability than our competitors. And I just want to give you an example of that. If you look at the latest MITRE results, Falcon had 98% coverage. Our next-gen competitor had 79% coverage. It took Falcon 4 minutes from mean time to detection. It took our competitor 47 minutes. And the list goes on and on. If we think about the architecture as well, we have a very lightweight agent, requires 100 megs of storage. Our competitor has a heavy agent, requires 3 gigabytes of storage in the Windows environment.
— George Kurtz, Founder, CEO & Director, CrowdStrike Q2 FY2025 earnings call, 2024-08-28
filingsupports
Our lightweight sensor is built to support Windows, Mac, and Linux operating systems. The sensor is hardened against attacks and uses a combination of kernel and user-mode modules to collect and transmit high fidelity endpoint events as they take place on a system. It correlates these events using a local situational model on the endpoint, analyzes via agent-based AI models and is capable of taking a variety of preventative and responsive actions on the endpoint, either automatically or via human control.
— CrowdStrike FY2026 Form 10-K, 2026-03-05
filingsupports
We built the Falcon platform entirely in and for the cloud, enabling collection and analysis of a massive, crowdsourced dataset from all of our customers to stop breaches. Our platform is designed to be redundant, resilient, and high-performing. Delivering security from the cloud enables agility, ease of use, and protection for workloads on a variety of endpoints wherever they are located. As customer adoption grows, the network effect of each additional endpoint added to the Falcon platform will amplify the breadth and depth of our dataset and intelligence.
— CrowdStrike FY2026 Form 10-K, 2026-03-05

presentationsupports
Well, you have to look at some of the other operating systems, and it's really important to realize that a Mac operating system is different than Linux world, is different than Windows. And Windows has a kernel structure where, like, they build 1 kernel and it supports all these different versions of Windows, right? And then there's a massive focus on backward compatibility. You can run really old programs on Windows 11. So it's just different. ... And there's really kind of 4 areas that are important. One is visibility of telemetry to get out of the kernel. Second one is the ability to block which you need to do on the kernel. The third one is anti-tamper protection. And then the fourth one is performance. This is actually why you run in the kernel and to realize that a lot of the attacks do take place in memory and you have to have that visibility.
— George Kurtz, Founder, CEO & Director, Goldman Sachs Communacopia & Technology Conference 2024, 2024-09-11

But keep in mind, there is a performance advantage of being able to run in the kernel, period. Why is it that CrowdStrike has the best performance in the market? Well, we've got the best architecture in the market. Why is it that we're the only technology that you can install without a reboot? Because we thought about it for many, many hard years.

To help our customers and partners increase resilience, we are developing new Windows capabilities that will allow security product developers to build their products outside of kernel mode. This means security products, like anti-virus solutions, can run in user mode just as apps do. This change will help security developers provide a high level of security, easier recovery, and there will be less impact to Windows in the event of a crash or mistake. A private preview will be made available for our security product ecosystem in July 2025.
— David Weston, Corporate Vice President, Enterprise and OS Security, Windows security and resiliency: protecting your business (Windows Experience Blog), 2024-11-19

Next month, we will deliver a private preview of the Windows endpoint security platform to a set of MVI partners. The new Windows capabilities will allow them to start building their solutions to run outside the Windows kernel. This means security products like anti-virus and endpoint protection solutions can run in user mode just as apps do. This change will help security developers provide a high level of reliability and easier recovery resulting in less impact on Windows devices in the event of unexpected issues. We will continue to collaborate deeply with our MVI partners throughout the private preview.
— David Weston, Corporate Vice President, Enterprise and OS Security, The Windows Resiliency Initiative: Building resilience for a future-ready enterprise (Windows Experience Blog), 2025-06-26

Peer: The Stack (trade press)

websupports
Microsoft is gearing up to nudge EDR vendors out of the Windows kernel - a move that follows CrowdStrike's failure to spot a bug that caused its agent (running in kernel mode) to crash over eight millions computers. Redmond will release a new Windows endpoint security platform in private preview next month that will see "security-product developers" build their products outside of kernel mode," it confirmed on June 25. That's part of an architectural overhaul that Microsoft is making much of being a collaborative industry effort with multiple EDR vendors: It names Bitdefender, CrowdStrike, ESET, SentinelOne, Trellix, Trend Micro, and WithSecure as members of its "Windows Resiliency Initiative" (WRI).
— Microsoft to nudge EDR out of the kernel, 2025-06-30

Smart filtering is one of the things that we have in our technology which others don't. And it's the ability, you can think about it as a camera aperture. When those workloads or endpoint agents are seeing something that might be anomalous, they can dynamically open and close. ... And that allows us to get the information we need but also do it in a very cost-efficient and performant way. And most other competitors, if they have something, it's sort of a model that just either throws data up, which overwhelms the network and/or the cost structure is too high or they leave it on the endpoint and have to query it.

presentationsupports
Part of the secret sauce of the fact that we've got smart filtering technology that allows us to dynamically figure out what to take up to the cloud, there's always a baseline, but we can open and close that aperture. And that basically increase the performance on the endpoint. There really is no performance impact. It doesn't overburden the network, and it certainly allows us to increase our margins.
— George R. Kurtz, Co-Founder, CEO, President & Director, Bank of America Merrill Lynch 2020 Global Technology Conference, 2020-06-04

The owned-colocation model has not produced a gross-margin gap against the AWS-only peers. CrowdStrike's subscription gross margin of 78% GAAP compares with Okta at 80% and Elastic at 81%, both on AWS, and with Zscaler at 77% on its own colocation; the gap it has produced is against SentinelOne at 74%, whose cost base is AWS usage charges at a fifth of CrowdStrike's scale. Scale, module attach and smart filtering explain more of the 78-81% than colocation does.

The constraint now is contractual.
